member of the commission. − mr president, i very much welcome the successful conclusion of the negotiations on the 2014-2020 consumer programme. this might be one of the smaller mff programmes, amounting to around eur 189 million, but it will play an essential role as a catalyst for consumer policy at eu level by complementing and supporting national consumer policies. this will be achieved by financing a number of key actions under the consumer agenda – the eu strategy on consumer policy – and by laying the basis for meeting emerging challenges. the programme will focus on four specific objectives: consumer safety; consumer information and education, including support to consumer organisations; rights and redress; and last but not least, enforcement. i would like sincerely to thank parliament for its hard work and especially mr rochefort – the rapporteur for the committee on the internal market and consumer protection – and the shadow rapporteurs for their personal commitment to this file. during the negotiations, parliament emphasised the special importance of the consumer programme, given the direct impact on citizens of consumer policy. i fully share your assessment. the actions supported by the programme will contribute towards strengthening the confidence of eu consumers in the single market and empowering them to play a key role in the economy. ultimately, a high level of consumer protection will contribute to our overarching priority of making markets work better and supporting growth. i am reassured to see that parliament, the council and the commission share to a very large extent the same view of the objectives of the programme, its beneficiaries, and the actions it should finance. all three institutions were largely in agreement as to the substance of the programme. the negotiations served to improve the text and led to a number of adjustments in order properly to integrate the political priorities identified by parliament.we have made sure that actions to be financed under this programme are targeting areas where intervention at eu level can truly make a difference and add value. these include actions to further promote cooperation in the field of product safety, better coordination in enforcement, co-financing of the european consumer centres and support for eu-level representation of consumer interests. the few sensitive issues identified during the negotiations related rather to management of the programme. here i think that the solution we have found together is a balanced one. it fully recognises the prerogative of parliament but also makes sure that the programme is manageable and that procedures remain suitable for its small size. the evaluation scheduled for 2017 will offer the opportunity to review the implementation of the programme, to discuss it together and to make adjustments if necessary. it is important to highlight the fact that, in these times of financial constraints and scarce resources, the commission sought to preserve as far as possible the programmeʼs budget as initially proposed. a cut amounted to only around 4 %, reflecting the importance that the commission, like parliament, attaches to this small but important programme. following these long but successful negotiations, our priority now is to implement the programme as rapidly and efficiently as possible. we have indeed already started the technical work for 2014, pending the formal adoption and publication of the legal base, which we hope will be completed by march. we very much look forward to working closely with parliament when implementing the programme over the next seven years, to the benefit of european consumers.
on behalf of the ppe group. this is the first report that i have had the pleasure of following on behalf of the european people's party, and which has now been concluded. i would therefore like to thank mr rochefort, the representatives of the other parliamentary groups and our assistants for all their work and cooperation on this dossier. i am very pleased with the work we have done and will therefore give my full support to this report to spend the allocation of eur 189 million for improving consumer protection through measures to safeguard safety, remedies, education and enforcement of consumer rights. in particular, i welcome the improvement that will occur in the consumer price comparison sites. the report also stresses that there should be an evaluation of this programme and a scrutinising of the way the funds are allocated and used. finally, this report sends an important message to our constituents that the european parliament puts consumer protection at the top of its agenda. i am proud to give my support to the adoption of this report and i urge everyone in this house to do the same.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this regulation because i believe we should empower consumers. i am pleased that during the negotiations between the council and the commission, the european parliament insisted on the importance of initiatives to increase market transparency. in the future, consumers will be better placed to compare offers on products and services such as, for example, as we see in imco amendments nos 67 and 68. moreover, we should have the tools to identify shortcomings in order to address them in the interest of the consumer. it is essential that consumers are always in a position to exercise their rights under european laws as well as to have the right to defend their interests under the law. i am also pleased that, in carrying out this work, this parliament is aiming to help deliver the europe 2020 strategy.
we welcome the e-health action plan as we believe it is a useful tool for the health service both in the european union and in countries. the country, which for many years has been regarded as the mediterranean hospital for the important role it has always played in this sector, has a particular interest in this action plan. with the services the country offers, malta is becoming one of the popular choices for those seeking the highest quality healthcare. the health sector is developing at a very fast pace and needs all the support it can get to become more competitive. with technological investment, the new system should offer better standards for the health sector in the european context, providing best-in-class privacy and security. provisions should be made to ensure training and information so that everyone benefits from them in the most effective way. it is also necessary to take into account the fact that not every region in europe has the same level of technological advancement, and therefore we must ensure that there is an appropriate level everywhere in order to operate these systems effectively. to do this, we need to provide training in the regions that need it. the commission shall facilitate the european structural funds to help create a legal and administrative framework allowing the use of health-related data and information. i believe that this system can benefit everyone, including the elderly. this should help us to further combat the spread of disease in our society in the coming years, to the benefit of all, in particular the elderly. in my work in the european parliament i have always given due importance to the field of health, particularly as regards the needs of those suffering from the condition of dementia. we need to remember that access to a quality health system is a fundamental right of european citizens and therefore we believe that this e-health action programme should help our people to have a better life.
i voted in favour of this report, which highlights the important issue of social protection. the european welfare state, yes, the european way of life, is under severe pressure due to the economic crisis and the sharp cut in public spending on the one hand, and social challenges, such as an ageing population, on the other. this call to member states was therefore necessary to ensure that there is responsible and sustainable long-term funding of the social security system and that the european social model continues to be supported. this rule pays full attention to the special cases of self-employed persons. according to the 2012 data, the self-employed represent more than 15% of those who are self-employed in the european union. in the same year, there were 23 000 selfemployed persons in malta. we need to understand and recognise that the self-employed provide a form of work that helps to create jobs and reduce unemployment. especially for women, who in malta and the rest of europe are at a higher risk of becoming unemployed, the option of self-employment can help reduce poverty. on the other hand, we must also understand that in many cases of self-employed people, this option is not always their preferred one, but a necessity created by the lack of work opportunities or the lack of arrangements to make working hours more flexible and thus be able to cope with both employment and care work.
vice-president of the commission. − madam president, today’s debate clearly underlines the strong symbolic meaning of the greek presidency. it is the presidency of the country which, according to various cassandras, was supposed to abandon the common currency or even to leave our union; the presidency of the country which came back, which demonstrated enormous courage, and whose people are working hard to return to economic prosperity. the greek presidency is symbolic proof of european resilience, political determination and strong european solidarity. most of us taking part in this debate clearly recognise that greece has covered a lot of ground, and today we see a clear comeback. however, we know there have been particularly difficult moments, and i want to thank prime minister samaras for recognising the fact that the commission was at all times on the greek side, always looking for the best possible solution, and that for the commission – as for this parliament, i know – a greek exit was never an option. why? because we believed in greece, in the greek people and in european cooperation and solidarity. i cannot agree with certain statements i have heard today to the effect that the euro is a primary cause of greek economic problems. just look around you. other countries outside the european union, such as iceland, and many eu member states not in the eurozone face the same strong need for consolidation. i believe the greek people know better: their support for the euro has been at a high level throughout the crisis and is even stronger today. concerning the other topics raised today, and especially the single resolution mechanism, i would like to reiterate the commission’s readiness to work closely with this house and with the council to find a workable, realistic solution in order to ensure that the intergovernmental dimension is kept to the absolute minimum that is politically necessary. we must work hard to get this done before the european elections, because we need to complete the banking union. if you will allow me a last remark, madam president, i will mention the need to restore the financing of the real economy. this topic was raised by several speakers, and i absolutely agree with them. we should make better use of the new instruments developed by the commission and the european investment bank and better use of the eu budget, which is a true investment budget for the entire union. we can do this by implementing the multiannual financial framework quickly. prime minister, the commission is prepared to cooperate with you and with the greek presidency and hopes to achieve much success during your semester. having heard the conviction with which you spoke about europe, and knowing your determination, i am convinced that your presidency will be a great success.
, president-in-office of the council. − madam president, first of all i want to thank everyone. i want to thank the european parliament and president schultz for all their help and for the dedication they have shown to my country, and i want to thank you for the remarks and comments you have all made today. everything will be taken into account. i was told that i have only five minutes to answer your questions, so allow me to be as precise and fast as possible. i need not comment on remarks which stem from an inflexible or isolationist view of europe. i am here to work for europe, not against it. i would like to thank mr farage for all the lessons about what democracy is and what democracy is not. i would have appreciated it even more if, besides all the lecturing, there could have been some hint of solidarity. i saw none. (applause) we know the intergovernmental approach. we know the community approach. now we are becoming aware of the humorist approach. i enjoy a good joke, but i do not take it seriously. some may not be happy that greece did not give in to the exit scenario – that greece indeed delivered. i am sorry about this, but i honestly did not want to damage anybody’s anti-european agenda. (applause)since i heard a mention of golden dawn – which only shows that there is indeed a very thin line between unmasked populism and disguised extremism – i want to say that the prolonged recession with the unprecedented unemployment level, the gradual failing of law and order and the presence of hundreds of thousands of illegal immigrants were the three sources of the problem. what we have been doing since this government took office, however, has been to address and correct all three of those sources simultaneously, and as soon as normality is restored in greece the golden dawn phenomenon will be marginalised. in the meantime we are doing everything to reveal who these people really are. we do not forget that the real target is to expose them, not to victimise them. i also heard specific mention of an investment in skouries in chalkidiki. the reference was to an investment which fully complies with all environmental regulations – national, local and european – and which is now inhibited because of illegal extremist activities. democracy and foreign direct investment are not going to be left to the mercy of extremists in greece. we want investments, and when they are in accordance with european environmental measures those investments will take place. they are absolutely necessary when a country has the unemployment level that we have in greece. about banking union and the single resolution mechanism: as i said at the beginning, we started work on this immediately. i too want a real banking union. i know that parliament is demanding radical changes in what has been agreed so far, and we have to listen sincerely to the concerns of the european parliament. the top priority for our presidency is the trialogue on the single resolution mechanism, where productive negotiation has already started. to be honest, however, these negotiations are going to be very challenging and difficult. i do not say ‘futile’, but i do say ‘difficult’. on growth, jobs and cohesion, everybody understands that the current level of unemployment in europe is politically unsustainable. we must explore ways to restore normal lending to the real economy: that is among the most ambitious priorities of our presidency. we have to remain focused on all the parameters that affect lending to business, and especially financing for small and medium-sized enterprises (smes). as we see it, easing liquidity for europe’s smes, especially at the periphery and including young innovative companies, will help to boost employment for our youth and to create the conditions for sustainable and competitive growth. i appreciate what has been said about the initiative to reduce interest-rate differences, which joseph daul talked of, and about the need to avoid social dumping, as highlighted by mr swoboda. about the troika: i was very critical of some of the mistakes that were made at the beginning, during the first programme while i was in opposition, but even in opposition i was always trying to improve the programme and to push for reforms and deficit-cutting. i wish that i had that type of opposition in greece today. since then, we have corrected a series of mistakes and shortcomings, and now the programme has begun to yield results. as to how to deal with these problems in the future, we need to debate that seriously but, significantly, the fiscal problems we faced in greece are now in the past. we have already taken care of them. we are trying to conclude all these efforts in my country successfully but – please – there should be no ex-post political debate that makes our life more difficult. at the same time, i would say to some of the speakers today that each country has its own difficulties. sometimes they have to do with geography, so, on the question i was asked in relation to gdp about the 4 % defence spending which we still have, i would answer that the figure has indeed fallen – but please do not push me to suggest that you examine your own country’s geography versus my country’s geography. as far as cyprus is concerned, cyprus is in the middle of a negotiation to resolve 40 years of unlawful partition and foreign occupation. we want a just and viable solution as soon as possible, but i cannot make any public comments today regarding the on-going negotiations. irrespective of progress on the cyprus talks, however, all candidate countries should fully recognise and fully respect all the existing eu member states. this is not a political point and it is not about greece or cyprus: it is about our legal personality and our institutional integrity as a union. finally, i was asked about turkey. naturally, we follow the developments in that country very closely. despite many differences, both governments are trying to keep bilateral relations as calm as possible. we are trying to improve our cooperation on some neutral economic areas such as trade and tourism. we have improved our relationship in terms of combating terrorism. we also expect improvements in areas such as border control and the control of illegal immigration. we hope no internal problems in either country will be exported to the other country. this is what i wanted to say in the five minutes you gave me: i am sorry i went to ten. again, let me thank everybody for their presence here today. we greatly appreciate it, and we are doing what we can. as i said at the beginning, we are implementing what we have undertaken to implement and we are honouring our signature. we are delivering fruitful results, and we hope that greece, together with all the other european nations, will enter the next phase, which will be a phase not simply of recovery but of real growth, so that unemployment – our biggest concern – will fall in each one of our countries. thank you once again for allowing me to be here today. (applause)
in writing. greece is probably the most troubled country in the european union. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the new regulation to include the areas of the common fisheries policy, the common fisheries policy and the common fisheries policy. this also applies to millions of workers in other countries who are looking for work. it also counts for millions of citizens living in poverty or at risk of poverty. it is true that even in this parliament we have discussed these problems several times and have even taken steps and set targets, but this is not enough to win back the citizens' trust. to win back the trust of these one way there. we must work towards a social europe not just on paper, but a real social europe. a social europe means decent jobs for everyone who wants to work and can work. a social europe means a decent standard of living for all families. to achieve these goals, concrete steps need to be taken, as is being done in malta where we now have a government that is doing everything it can to create decent jobs and paying special attention to those at risk of poverty. i believe that greece has learned a lot from the experiences it has been going through, and i am therefore convinced that the greek presidency will work to move ever closer towards a truly social europe.
in writing. − it is obvious that we must always look beyond europe if we want europe to become economically stronger and help itself find a solution to the economic problems it is facing. therefore, any step taken to strengthen economic relations with third countries should have the support of all of us. but the citizens of these countries expect more from europe. they expect to enjoy the same rights as citizens in europe. they expect to start enjoying the same living standards as citizens in europe. it is therefore desirable that, in addition to the strengthening of economic relations, there should also be an improvement in political relations. we must not be satisfied with a paper agreement. we need to make sure that every agreement is followed up by the administrations of the countries involved, and this applies not only to economic relations, but also to political relations to make sure that all citizens benefit from these agreements.
in writing. − any step taken to spend tax money wisely is always a step in the right direction. to put it simply, when it comes to government-to-private contracts, it means not only value for money, but also consideration of the rights of workers and those who are expected to benefit from the contract. in malta this was a serious problem because we had contracts awarded to companies that had made the cheapest bid, but in reality they could do so because their plan was to get rich by exploiting their workers. this led to precarious work where workers of these same companies were not even paid the national minimum wage. in addition, they had no basic rights, such as leave and sick leave, or overtime pay for overworked hours. i would also like to mention the exploitation of immigrants not only in malta but also in other countries in europe. it is a well-known secret that companies that are given government contracts without any scruples are served by the misfortunes of these immigrants. it is worth noting that the maltese government, as it was entrusted with the running of the country last march, took immediate steps to put an end to these abuses and exploitation of workers once and for all. i hope that governments in other countries will take similar steps.
the maltese people have worked hard to get malta into the european union. we are proud of it. proud to be maltese and proud to be european. we are a people who have been chosen to work for our rights and know what our obligations and duties are. this subject has been controversial. the majority of maltese neither agreed nor wanted the sale of our citizenship. i and others who spoke out against this scheme were called traitors. we are one people, one calibre, and this scheme is not representative of us. we never got there! but as a representative of the maltese people, i cannot remain closed-minded. it is my duty to speak out and no political attack will stop me. we are talking about the sale of citizenship that is being done with almost no strings attached and no requirement for the person to stay in the country but with all the benefits that citizenship brings. this parliament is also our parliament, the maltese parliament. we are elected by the people to represent them. let's be clear: our rights are not all for sale! the sale of citizenship is reduced by its true value and it threatens and destroys the rights of each and every one of us. as vice-president reding said, being a citizen of a country means having a full and deep connection with that country. now, a person can become a maltese and european citizen without that link to the union. this goes against the values, trust and solidarity which are the foundation of the union and its future. we are not diminishing the concept of sovereignty; is because we believe in the european project that we want a europe built on strong rights. i would like to conclude by making another call for consensus: there is still time to reform this scheme. (the president agreed to answer the question referred for a preliminary ruling (rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure))
answer to blue paper. thank you very much, i thank mr melo for asking me this question. the title of this resolution is on the sale of citizenship: the sale of maltese citizenship and the scheme the socialist government is proposing is not limited or is not a genuine link to the investment. it is on this that i have made my intervention, it is on this that the resolution we are going to vote on tomorrow will focus.
among other things, this motion before us speaks of european values. yes, we are talking about core values that unite us as europeans, we are talking about mutual solidarity between member states, we are talking about respect for the sovereignty of all member states in areas of national competence. we need to concentrate on the true principle of citizenship and to do this we must avoid any prejudice towards any one member state. this is a moment that requires all of us to be mature and serious and therefore let us be prudent. it is not fair to concentrate on one member state. if that were to happen, the game would be dangerous and would no longer be in the common european interest. we are also setting a precedent on the sovereignty of the member states. as europeans we must make a choice between a european economy that opens up to the rest of the world or a european economy that shuts itself off. we must have the courage to open the doors to those who want to invest in europe; investment that grows economies and creates jobs. it is satisfying to see a country like malta, which is economically successful, while not burdening the less successful with more taxes. in malta as a country we believe in sincere solidarity. we have always shown this with facts in the case of irregular migration. we should always do so. let us open the discussion but not open the doors to opportunities that bring benefits, prosperity and economic growth, and this in the context of european values.
we have a debate before us which is not a debate against the maltese people, nor is it a debate against the maltese government, it is a debate which speaks of a scheme being introduced in countries. malta, now a member of the european union, has for a number of years been part of schengen, part of the euro area and is proud of the obligations we also have towards the european union and the duties that membership is supposed to bring with it. and so we don't want you to now because of this evil scheme start to suspect us. i want you to trust him when he comes to malta or gozo with a passport and not to have suspicions about the person carrying a maltese passport. and that is why i am saying today that we still have a chance to hear from the maltese government about this consensus. i was pleased to hear my colleague from the socialist party, the green party, the communist party, the people's party. you have consent here to the maltese government i say to listen and change the scheme and make it an investment one. because i agree with everything that was said here tonight, including by my socialist party colleague göncz, who said that european citizenship is not for sale. so let's put it all together, let's find common rules to ensure that neither today's nor tomorrow's governments sell european citizenship in this way. let's make guidelines, let's see what needs to be done to see together how best to defend european citizenship. and there has to be a real link, there has to be a real connection for you to get a passport. if you entrust me, and my country, malta and gozo, with the keys to your house, which is now our house, i cannot go and take that key and pass it on to everyone, without your permission, without the council's permission, without the commission's permission! i regret that countries have not communicated with the commission, the member states or this parliament, but this is a scheme and it is not against any government or the maltese people. question for the blue paper. i am sorry to ask a question to a colleague from the same country, but i cannot help but ask him since he has referred to the consensus on this scheme confirms, the honourable member, that the maltese socialist government, led by prime minister dr joseph muscat, has not only consulted widely with all the social groups in our country but also with the opposition led by the leader of the opposition who my colleague, or david casa, represents from the same party? so open is the maltese socialist government to the discussion on this scheme!
answer to blue paper. i, mr president, just heard commissioner viviane reding today making comments outside, quoted even in the newspapers, where she said she did not know of any consultation with the commission and, to her knowledge, no consultation had been made with the member states. i ask my colleague from the socialist party to check his facts because if he has a problem with this, i think he should take it up with commissioner reding and not with me.
mr president, i am going to speak in english, as madame reding, the commissioner, chose that language in which to address us. i followed her with very keen interest. why? because she made a legal argument. if you take away all the political rhetoric, you will realise that her main items of objection are legal. she accepted the fact that citizenship is subject to national jurisdiction, but she also said that according to the maastricht treaty – and she is right – there has to be a direct link. in other words, the objection is not whether money is paid or not. the objection is whether there is a direct link or not. let us crystallise the problem and not try and make a scapegoat of the smallest country in the european union.
i will not go into the merits of the case because i do not agree with the fact that a decision of the democratically elected maltese parliament is coming to the scrutiny and vote of another parliament on an internal matter. as dr simon busuttil himself said in this parliament, it is a dangerous precedent, the european parliament starts interfering in the internal affairs of the member states. i have no doubt that none of my colleagues in the house will accept that it be done to their country. the granting of citizenship has been and is carried out by other member states; is a fundamental right of each member state. the methodology, the reason for the grant, is not a matter for this parliament, and therefore i find that this parliament is going beyond its competence. i ask you: why was this debate not held when other member states entered similar schemes? it's all about money. why was there no question of european values at that time? why now with malta: the smallest member country? we remind you that malta is a small country but has the same rights as any other state and it is unacceptable to interfere with its sovereignty as well. i would remind those who are instigating this debate that malta is no longer a colony and those who are using this parliament to humiliate malta, for partisan political purposes, shame on them!
i have a point of order because at the beginning of this debate, when you spoke and explained what this debate is about, you said that this is a debate on malta. in fact this is a debate on citizenship, and so i want to clarify what you said and that is why i made this point of order; because although i too wanted to speak in this debate, i was not given the chance. i would also like to say, to clarify what you have said because this is a debate on citizenship, that there is no problem - indeed we want a healthy debate on citizenship in the european union, bearing in mind that there is national sovereignty - but we cannot capture a country and isolate it when there are other countries with similar schemes and initiatives, and therefore i invite this house, mr president, to, firstly, recognize that this is a debate on citizenship, and secondly i also urge this house to have a reasonable approach to the whole debate we have before us.

in writing. − i voted against this resolution because i could never accept that reference to malta should remain in the citizenship motion in the european parliament. in fact i even voted to remove all reference to malta from this motion. with today's vote i voted in favour of malta. i acknowledge and appreciate the fact that the socialist and democratic group tabled amendments to remove malta from the joint motion for a resolution. i regret that the opposition in countries used this parliament to try to gain political advantage through this motion. the purpose of this parliament is to discuss matters of interest to citizens, not to be used by some members for partisan purposes. i would like to clarify that any objection to discussing citizenship at european level should be removed. what is worrying is that a debate suddenly arises because a country - in this case malta - has started an investment initiative whose proceeds will go to benefit citizens, with direct investment in education, research, health and the environment among others.
in writing. − i voted wholeheartedly against this joint motion for a resolution on citizenship for sale, because i am convinced that the european union, as an actor that promotes the values of european solidarity, cooperation and mutuality, should not restrict or focus on one member state’s proposal, when similar schemes already exist within the eu. why should the eu erect barriers to a member state that is striving to find ways to help its economy prosper without burdening its citizens? malta has done its duty towards the eu’s values and will continue to do so. when it came to issues such as immigration, malta showed solidarity and provided help. in return, this resolution is an act against giving malta the opportunity to become more prosperous and competitive. this case should not be considered or raised to serve as a precedent in the future as it might be dangerous and cause controversies. the eu is about co-operation and respect, and such a resolution is against these values among many others. thus, it is an unjust obstacle to malta’s economic development and will only serve to create an unfair and untrue bad image of malta that could have negative implications on its economy in the future.
in writing. − the fact that accession negotiations between the eu and serbia will soon start is a step in the right direction. at the same time efforts on combating child poverty and standing up to violent groups attacking lgbti people is essential. it needs to be clear that discrimination, on whatever grounds, is unacceptable.
in writing. − it is obvious that when a country has to take drastic measures to meet serious economic and financial problems, there are repercussions that affect the lives of millions of citizens. a number of european countries are currently in this situation and a significant number of families are ending up homeless because they have lost their jobs and therefore literally have no money to pay rent. it is unfortunate and a bit hurtful to see people sleeping outside in major european cities. but this problem is not limited to those who sleep outside. it is also about those who are given shelter in some home or other, and have to accept this shelter because they cannot do otherwise, lest they end up sleeping outdoors. the right to housing is not just a roof over your head. it means having a decent home to live in, where you can live without relying on the mercy of a landlord. it is therefore appropriate that this problem is being raised in the european parliament and it is also appropriate to call for a european strategy to address this problem. my appeal is to understand the problem and start addressing it today before tomorrow.
i note and welcome the agreement and understanding reached between the maltese government led by prime minister joseph muscat and the european commission on the citizenship by investment scheme which the maltese authorities have called for. there were no major differences between malta's position and that of the european commission and there was an immediate deadlock. this is a shocking achievement. this is a satisfactory agreement for all. over the last few weeks there has been a lot of misinformation about this scheme. this was the fault of certain members of this parliament who preferred to hinder their country for partisan political gain, rather than working for consensus. there were those who wanted to eliminate this scheme. malta is not the only country with such a scheme. other investment citizenship schemes exist in other eu member states. the main stakeholders in the country agree with this programme, the european commission agrees as well as the people of malta. this scheme will benefit the people of malta, as well as the european union. the agreement also shows that the attack on malta in last month's plenary session was extreme, and above all unnecessary.
this report sends a clear and important message to our societies that discrimination against lgbti people is unacceptable. in recent months, as the spokesperson for my group on this report along with ms lunacek and other colleagues, we have been engaged in useful discussions to present a balanced document that makes each of us proud of our contribution. the report is in line with the role and competence of the european union, respects the principle of subsidiarity and in no way infringes the right of each member state to legislate on matters which are within its national competence. a quick look at what does not make this report: no new laws, no country will be obliged to legislate on some new form of family, no new special privileges, no provisions on positive discrimination in favour of one sector or another, it will not turn the world upside down. however, what this report will do is to begin the road to full equality for all. there have been reports against reports that all say there is discrimination against lgbti people. our call to the commission is to create a roadmap, a strategy, an action plan to tackle discrimination. i will therefore give my support to the calls and appeals made in this report and encourage everyone to do the same. thank you.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the renewal of the protocol between the eu and the gabonese republic on fisheries. the new protocol aims to give european vessels the opportunity to fish in the waters of the gabonese republic, within the limits of the available surplus, in accordance with the available scientific advice and the recommendations of the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas (iccat). the two sides also aim to improve the periodic monitoring of catches in order to preserve the stock and protect tuna and sea health. the annual financial contribution paid by the european union to gabon for the fishing of tuna and other migratory species is eur 1 350 000.

in writing. - the arms trade treaty (att) is a major step forward. with around 740 000 men, women and children dying every year because of illegal and poorly regulated arms trade, we need to combat this on a global level. the eu should play a leading role in promoting the treaty and ensuring that no more weapons get through to unstable regions and fall in the hands of terrorists. member states should therefore ratify this as soon as possible. even though ammunition and certain types of weapons are not yet within the scope of the treaty, this is an important step, as it will clearly prohibit transfer of arms in cases where a un arms embargo is in place for instance, or when there are known risks of genocide, crimes against humanity or war crimes. global recognition of the illegality of arms trade in this case is crucial, and it is in the interest of all citizens around the world that the att will be internationally ratified.

in writing. - i support this report, which calls for a directive of the european parliament and of the council on the harmonisation of the laws of the member states relating to electromagnetic compatibility. this directive is part of a package, aligning nine product directives to the new legislative framework created in 2008, which improves consistency of application of european union law in line with the changes brought in by the lisbon treaty and increases the confidence of both producers and consumers.
today, the european parliament voted on one of the most important reports of this legislation. today's vote is a victory for all citizens and consumers of the european union, because it will have a direct impact on their lives. this is due to the hard work of this institution despite often being considered as far away from the everyday life of the people. the report on passenger rights will be seen as a tool to meet the challenges of the twenty-first century relating to air traffic and which can help to protect passenger rights vis-à-vis large airlines. in a liberalised european market, where both the number of flights and the number of passengers are increasing, we need to ensure that passengers' rights are guaranteed. with regard to the handling of complaints, it is important to introduce a complaints procedure with a clear time limit and precise identification of the new role of the entity to be established for the handling of complaints. in baggage allowances, passengers shall have the right to carry in the cabin at no cost essential personal items which also include airport purchases in addition to the maximum allowance for baggage. the improvements include more transparent information, more accessible contact points at the airport and an easier complaints procedure and strengthening in the enforcement system. the conditions on baggage rules have been better harmonised and it will no longer be possible to refuse a passenger to board a return journey because the passenger missed the initial journey. our duty as members of the european parliament was to ensure a coherent perspective by providing a clear understanding of citizens' rights. this is without allowing citizens in situations where the airline companies take advantage of their position.
in writing. - lack of information, lack of compensation, and lack of assistance to air passengers are amongst the main concerns of consumers in their (inter)national travels. it is important that these concerns have now finally been tackled by the proposed regulation. on the one hand, this provides legal certainty for both travellers and airlines, but more importantly, it strengthens the rights of passengers and protects them against unfair practices. people who face long delays, for instance, will enjoy the same right to compensation as when a flight is cancelled. also, there will be more clarity on what kind of hand luggage can be carried on board and, overall, passengers can expect more assistance in case of delays and cancellations. we need this kind of comprehensive approach when it comes to the rights of citizens when travelling in the eu, and therefore i gladly voted in favour of this report.

member of the commission. - madam president, i would like to thank you for putting female genital mutilation (fgm) on the agenda today. no girl should ever have to experience this traumatic breach of her rights. i would also like to thank the parliament and the committee on women’s rights and gender equality for their commitment to combatting violence against women, and fgm in particular. every day in europe, women are raped or beaten, or die, just because they are women. this is not acceptable and nor is the fact that millions of young women are still subjected to genital mutilation every year. they risk death, and if they survive their injury they face the prospect of disability and very risky childbirth. the european union will continue to campaign for the rights and empowerment of women in all contexts by fighting gender-based violence and supporting relevant initiatives against this abhorrent crime. the commission will continue developing targeted and consistent initiatives to support member states in achieving tangible results in eradicating all forms of violence against women – making full use of european union competences. as far as non-member states are concerned, women’s and girls’ rights form an integral part of the commission’s deliberations when it comes to assessing what type of budget support is appropriate in a third country. but it does not end with this: it does not end simply with money. our efforts are about building global consensus. all recent european union trade agreements have been linked to the relevant political framework agreement governing the union’s overall relations with third countries, or incorporated into broader association agreements, and these framework or association agreements include a clause establishing respect for human rights as an essential element underpinning relations, as well as a comprehensive cooperation pillar establishing channels for dialogue on a broad range of issues. honourable members, let us continue our close cooperation on this matter and let us use every opportunity to make our case. i will consult my colleague commissioner reding concerning all useful suggestions, including the one made by ms hall earlier today.

through the europe 2020 strategy, the european union has set itself the target of reducing by at least 20 million the number of people at risk of poverty or social exclusion by the year 2020. the economic and financial crisis, however, is spreading poverty and social exclusion across the union. some 120 million european citizens, an increase of six million in two years and today almost a quarter of the population, were at risk of poverty or social exclusion in 2011. more than 40 million suffer from some form of material deprivation. more than 25 million children in the union were at risk of poverty and social exclusion by 2011. at the same time, the economic and financial crisis has hampered the ability of member states to offer support to those at risk of poverty and social exclusion. for this reason, i am proud today that the socialists and democrats in the european parliament have taken the initiative to create a new fund to provide aid to those most in need. the fund of €2. the 5 billion proposed for the period 2014-2020 will serve as a larger instrument through which to reach out to needy people or families in member states whose need for assistance is established by national authorities or partner organisations, or indirectly through the provision of food and other needs to homeless people or children. i believe that the fund for european aid to the most deprived is a sign of european solidarity with those most affected by the crisis, even if the budget increase is still far from sufficient. the support expected from this proposed fund should not be considered as a substitute for a comprehensive policy that is necessary to reduce and end poverty. this is the challenge that the union and the member states still have to face in the future.
in writing. - one quarter of europeans – 120 million people –were at risk of poverty and social exclusion in 2010. european solidarity is essential. the fund for aid to the most deprived (fead) is a sign of such solidarity with those hit hardest by the crises. the budget of about eur 3.5 billion is still far from being enough. whilst a budget to help those most deprived is a step in the right direction, it is to be noted that it is a shame that the european union has a quarter of europeans at the risk of poverty and social exclusion. member states should be leaders in eradicating poverty and social exclusion.
in writing. - there is a need for continued eu support in order to continue to contribute towards alleviating some of the worst forms of poverty and social exclusion in europe, which is why i support this report, which proposes a fund that would focus on the people most at risk of material deprivation and food deprivation, particularly the homeless, children and households with children. this fund is critical to the achievement of the eu’s target of reducing the number of people at risk of poverty or social exclusion by at least 20 million by 2020.
in writing. - the fead is a vital instrument that has been created after much spearheading by the s&d. i wholeheartedly agree with the aims of this fund and thus voted in favour.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which seeks to make necessary corrections and clarifications to regulation no[nbsp ]528/2012 concerning the making available on the market and use of biocidal products. it is important that these technical errors be corrected as soon as possible in order to provide member states, industry and the general public with the highest degree of legal clarity and certainty.
in writing. - the report on biocidal products is a good report that combines high levels of health and environmental protection with some clear rules to promote innovation and competitiveness. the compromise reached by the rapporteur is satisfying and to this end, i am voting in favour of this report.
almost 45 per cent of women in europe have suffered some form of violence and up to 15 per cent have been victims of domestic violence. seven women in the european union die every day from domestic violence. serious action is needed to address this situation. many member states say they are unable to allocate more resources to combat this type of violence. but i believe it makes more sense to invest in preventive measures. today in the european union there is no law establishing measures to promote action in the member states in the field of preventing violence against women. a number of resolutions have been passed calling for action against this type of violence. i regret to note that the commission responded by saying that there is no legal basis for a directive. we therefore have neither a law nor a comprehensive strategy to combat violence against women. for this reason, i welcome this report which we are approving. this is a report with a resolution that also includes a legislative request whereby we are recommending not only that violence be identified in the minimum standards directive on victims' rights but also to create a forum for civil society and authorities concerned.
in writing. - violence against women, as a form of gender-based discrimination, should be condemned under all circumstances. at a time when one in four women has experienced physical violence at least once during their adult lives and 12 to 15 percent of women in europe are victims of domestic violence, we need to stop this once and for all. violence against women makes them vulnerable, and enhances gender inequality by hindering women’s full participation in economic, social, political, and cultural life; let alone the impact it has on their physical and psychological well-being. it is crucial that the european union takes the lead in the fight against violence against women and provides a legal framework to support member states in this.
in writing. - the fight against violence should be at the forefront of the ep’s work. violence against women is particularly heinous due to it social implications. to this end, i have voted in favour of this report that aims to combat these crimes.

on behalf of the s&d group. report approved today on the european semester for economic policy coordination employment and social aspects in the annual growth survey 2014 is being conducted at a time when unemployment in the european union has reached 26. 6 million people while youth unemployment reached 23 per cent in the european union. at the same time, almost a quarter of the european population is at risk of poverty. i am proud as a european socialist to say that we have achieved a satisfactory result with this report. for the first time, the joint employment report includes a table identifying social problems linked to work. i believe that this table should also include other indicators, in particular on child poverty, access to health care, as well as employment indices allowing an analysis of the social situation in the member states. the best way to create jobs is by increasing productive investment. we are therefore urging member states to create an investment package that will allow for improvements in the labour market especially in the interest of young people. the competitiveness of the european union is strengthened by investing more in education, research and development and the digital domain. i am concerned to see that some 20 member states are reducing education expenditure in relative terms. i therefore call on the commission to create a plan to help these member states improve their education spending. unused structural funds should go to a youth employment programme as well as to help small enterprises. member states should present a plan on how they will create jobs in those areas which have potential such as health, social care, industry and telecommunications. i stress the importance of giving priority to the youth guarantee. according to the ilo, eur[nbsp ]21[nbsp ] billion is needed to implement a programme to combat youth unemployment in the euro area. the european union and the member states shall give priority to allocating the funds necessary to carry out this programme.
in writing. - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - no economic progress should be considered 'progress' if the wealth created is not distributed in a fair way that reaches everyone. the creation of new jobs should therefore be considered as the basis of all economic progress because it is work that provides a decent living for all families. but when we talk about new jobs, we must not just talk about numbers. jobs are required to be quality jobs with decent wages and working conditions. jobs without adequate wages and working conditions are more exploitation than jobs. precarious work must never be tolerated. it is not easy to create decent jobs for everyone overnight. we need to plan for the long term in the hope of avoiding the crises we have had in recent years and the basis of all planning should be education and training. only then can we also succeed in the fight against poverty, especially child poverty.

in writing. the youth guarantee has the noble purpose of helping young people find decent work. by decent work i do not mean only jobs with a good salary, but also jobs with appropriate working conditions. we exploit young people by leaving them no choice but to accept precarious employment simply because they cannot do otherwise. hence the importance of this guarantee to offer young people who finish their education, and those who lose their jobs, the opportunity of alternative employment, or further education and training to eventually help them find decent employment. this guarantee is very much needed when we know that youth unemployment is enormous, so much so that in some countries it even exceeds 50%, but it will not work miracles, especially in the difficult times we are still going through. we must recognize that these difficult times are having their effects not only on young people. the millions of unemployed european citizens are not all young, and it would be a great failure on our part if we did not offer our support to other unemployed citizens. it would be a failure on our part not to show solidarity with these citizens, many of whom are likely to be heads of households, and then the consequences of being unemployed could be even more dire. it is good to give priority to the problem of young people because young people are the future, but the present also exists.

on behalf of the s&d group. the health for growth programme which came at a time of many challenges linked to globalisation and the weak economic situation which is having a strong negative effect on the european union. in this context, i welcome this third multiannual eu health programme for 2014-2020, as it means that the health and well-being of the european citizen is therefore seen as a sound investment in its future. with this important programme we will be strengthening, supporting and adding value to member states' policies to improve the health of eu citizens and reduce inequalities in the health sector by fostering innovation, improving quality of care and protecting the public from cross-border diseases and other health threats. it should be argued that the intention is that in this time of economic crisis in europe, health should be something more than simply and synonymously linked to a financial deficit. reform of the healthcare system is needed to bring expenditure under control, to bring about more innovation and to achieve greater profitability. only in this way can the european union meet the demand for more care brought by an ageing population and continue to provide the highest quality care for future generations. by improving the health of europe's older people, we will reduce the suffering of old age and lighten the burden on society in terms of the costs of care, protection and medical assistance. in my view, the programme should focus more on neurodegenerative diseases such as alzheimer's and other forms of dementia. this disease affects a large number of people - more than 7 million in europe suffer from some form of the disease - and has serious consequences for healthcare, the economy and society as a whole. the programme should therefore pay more attention to old age and its associated diseases. some governments have been more active than others in meeting these challenges. the government of malta, for example, is leading the way in how to have a structure through which those most in need can find the support they need. finally, i would argue that improving the health of people in the eu is essential. we must keep in mind the definition of the world health organisation: health is a complete state of physical, mental and social well-being and not merely the absence of disease or infirmity. other

in writing. - i support this report, which proposes regularly updating the lists of third countries whose nationals must be in possession of visas when crossing the external borders of member states and those who nationals are exempt from that requirement. i support the proposed changes being made to the lists
in writing. - it is true that thanks to the agreement reached, the violence and even the killing of protesters, who had the courage to take to the streets to listen to the voice of the majority of the people of ukraine, stopped. the formation of a coalition government has raised new hopes for the entire people of ukraine. they are also ambitious expectations given that the people are rightly hoping for significant changes in the short term to reduce abuses, corruption and discrimination. the challenges facing the government are therefore very great. more so when one considers the economic situation of the country. for our part, we must assure the people of ukraine that we will continue to stand shoulder to shoulder with them so that eventually all the goals of the same people become a reality.
in writing. - the situation in ukraine is a very dramatic one. i voted in favour of this resolution in order to support a peaceful resolution of the conflict.
in writing. - according to casualty figures released by the united nations assistance mission for iraq on 1 february 2014, a total of 733 iraqis were killed and another 1[nbsp ]229 were wounded in acts of terrorism and violence in january 2014, and these figures do not include casualties resulting from the ongoing fighting in anbar province. i support this resolution on the current situation in iraq because i believe that these statistics and the deteriorating state of security in iraq are deeply concerning and that the eu and the un must play an important role in helping iraqi citizens reduce violence by promoting democracy, human rights, good governance and the rule of law.
in writing. - i support this resolution on the use of armed drones. i believe that there needs to be an in-depth discussion at the european level regarding a common eu policy and position on the use of these systems.
in writing. - the michel report on fundamental rights in the european union is a very good and valid report. it is a broad report which manages to touch upon all those issues regarding what rights we expect for our citizens in 2014. from lgbti rights to issues of sexual violence on women to the banning of female genital mutilation, this report is a strong step forward. however, this report finds me perplexed with regard to other issues, particularly the issue of sexual and reproductive health rights. it is my idea that such ideas should not be included in such a report as i am against abortion. to this end, i have decided to abstain on this vote.
in writing. - i have voted in favour of this report, which seeks to improve the european arrest warrant framework decision of 2002 that made it much quicker and easier to bring suspects and accused persons to justice by transforming the old extradition procedure into a faster system through communication between judicial authorities. this decision has ensured that open borders are not exploited by those seeking to escape justice. however, there is still a lot of room for improvement in terms of this policy, which is why i support this report to address problems that have arisen with regard to the european arrest warrant framework decision.
in writing. - the european arrest warrant is an important tool for the creation of a safer europe. to this end i have voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - it is important for the eu to condemn all violence from all sides in venezuela and to urge the authorities to respect fundamental rights, constructive and respectful dialogue and tolerance in that country. only this can help overcome the serious crisis and put an end to social inequalities.
in writing. - women play an important role in the fisheries sector, not least those married to fishermen, because even if they are not officially considered as employees, directly or indirectly, they make a great and valuable contribution. it is therefore appropriate to give legal and social recognition to their work. i also believe that more women should be encouraged to enter this field and to this end we should work to improve working conditions so that employment in this field is decent with a decent income for everyone.
in writing. - i support this report which discusses private copying levies and their role in helping right holders achieve compensation for the use of their works. these levies are critical in ensuring that artists’ rights are protected and that they are fairly compensated.
in writing. – the report on equality between women and men is a laudable one. it treats the aspect of gender equality from all facets. it is unbelievable that even in the western world there is still the need to emphasise those areas were gender equality does not exist. of course in many other regions of the world the situation is worse and needs to be addressed in a balanced way. unfortunately the report includes references to reproduction issues and in particular to abortion. as is known throughout the european union, malta is adverse to these issues. the two political parties which dominate the political scene, the church and non-governmental organisations are against abortion under any circumstances. in view of this i will have to vote against the articles referring to this subject. against my will, i will also have to vote against the report as a whole. the reason is because, when the s&d malta delegation votes against specific clauses on abortion but then votes in favour of the report, it is unbelievable how we are criticised.
we are meeting today exactly two days after women's day. i would like to take this opportunity to remember not only maltese and gozitan women and european women, but also women around the world. i speak of this day with a certain sadness. because while i recognize that there have been women who have made a success of their lives, i also recognize that there are others who have not had the same opportunities. because that girl who lives in that remote corner on that island in the middle of the mediterranean, what opportunities could she have if she doesn't have access to education for so long? at the same time, how can that child, now a man, not be abused by his wife or partner if he is not taught that domestic violence does not make you a man? i also want to acknowledge and honour all those women who, because it was their choice and not by force, decided to make a career of caring for their families. they too need all the help and respect. how much help and respect do all these women need who, despite their careers, and even their disabilities, have decided to take care of their elderly parents instead of sending them to institutions. we therefore need policies and structures that really help women. we need to look at all of this holistically and so it requires a collective effort in one work to see that our goal is to develop a just society.
the maltese government has recently decided to place a gas storage tanker anchored in the heart of marsaxlokk bay. i coordinated a petition signed by thousands of maltese and gozitan citizens who are concerned. we want the european commission to protect the residents of the south of malta and investigate the dangers of the government's decision. we believe that the way the storage will be done is against the seveso directive. we want to know what the government, with its project, is doing to the residents, the environment, businesses, aquaculture and fishermen and their vessels. what the government wants to do is the complete opposite of what other european union governments have done, where they have taken the necessary precautions and kept the stockpile off the ground. an expert in this field said that if there is a gas leak, the consequences would be disastrous, and 91% of residents agree with him. we all want cleaner air and cheaper bills, but we believe that this can be done without the government putting residents at risk.
on behalf of the s&d group. other the events of recent days have further confirmed the importance of the european union, frightened and perhaps even depressed by the serious economic difficulties it is experiencing, not turning its back on those countries and peoples which are neighbours. i have said and i will continue to say that it would be a grave mistake for the european union to reduce its involvement, including financial involvement, in the mediterranean region. we all know what this region means in the history of mankind; we all know how sensitive the mediterranean is: large parts of it are still going through periods of instability and even adjustment. this parliament is called upon to give a signal. we must show that we have understood and that we have understood that internal prosperity also depends on external stability. it is therefore essential that the european union engages fully and strongly in the development of its surrounding regions. if the european union really wants to be relevant, it cannot abandon these peoples by reducing the level of its active participation. if we do, i believe history will judge us and even condemn us.

in writing. - the proposal is based on setting common eu-wide minimum standards for vehicle checks, with member states free to go further and introduce higher standards, if appropriate. the text adopted in the report at first reading in plenary changes the frequency of testing and endorses the commission’s proposal to make roadworthiness tests mandatory for motorcycles. therefore, new rules will apply on roadworthiness testing with the aim of further enhancing road safety and ensuring a high level of environmental protection.

i supported and voted in favour of this proposal, which aims to enforce car tests in order to increase road safety and strengthen environmental protection. before a vehicle is placed on the market it must be ascertained that it meets the appropriate levels of safety and environmental protection in the interest of every european citizen. each member state of the european union shall register each new car on the basis of a certificate of conformity issued by the manufacturer. this is to serve as insurance for cars on the road. this proposal talks about a clearer definition of the place of registration of cars while introducing the abolition of registration certificates. this allows cars that pose a road hazard to be decertified until they are subjected to another test. i believe that this directive is positive and that it is helpful for european citizens. this directive will also be able to cancel the registration of cars that are re-registered in another country. this is to remove the double registration of cars. scrapped cars are also deregistered. as i have said many times, the problems associated with taxes on the registration of cars manufactured in the member states represent an obstacle for citizens when exercising the right to the single market and go against this spirit. as i mentioned in my question to the european commission, the car registration tax is still creating obstacles for businesses and citizens despite the single market rules. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful organisms.
in writing. - this report, as part of the roadworthiness package, defines the place of registration of vehicles and introduces the withdrawal and the cancellation of registration certificates.
in writing. - i have voted in favour of this report, which is part of the overall ‘roadworthiness package’ that aims to improve road safety and environmental protection. this specific report seeks to amend council directive 1999/37/ec on the registration documents for vehicles. the changes made would improve the enforcement of the roadworthiness testing and roadside inspection regime by allowing authorities to temporarily withdraw or permanently cancel a vehicle’s registration if the technical condition of a vehicle creates a great risk to road safety.
in writing. - it is important that registration documents for vehicles are as indicated in the legislative resolution. i voted in favour.

in writing. - i voted in favour because i believe that the roadworthiness of commercial vehicles will lead to greater safety.

in writing. the european council's agenda for this month includes climate and energy. i take this opportunity to recall what the ec said to me when, in a parliamentary question, i asked about energy poverty. the reply stated that at eu level there are no statistics on the number of people considered to be in poverty or on a concept to protect them. it was stated that there is no obligation on member states to collect and report harmonised statistics on energy vulnerability and poverty among consumers. it was stated that the commission is conducting a study to identify vulnerability patterns in key consumer markets, including energy and that it should be published in 2015. i urge the european council to take account of energy poverty, at least at a generic level. women are particularly affected as the austerity measures in recent years have continued to weigh on them and their families. in a traditional society, which still exists in many european union member countries, many women work at home without financial compensation. we cannot forget that. it is not because women in employment cannot also end up energy poor, because after all i want to remember that many women are in precarious employment, and therefore, the strength of [their] money is limited. in the light of all this, i would therefore urge the european council to include gender in its strategies and policies. only then can we have a just society and a social europe.
in writing. - this resolution turns the spotlight on the social and economic situation in pakistan. pakistan has one of the highest out-of-school populations in the world, with an estimated 12 million children not attending school and about two thirds of pakistani women and half of pakistani men being illiterate. the country is still ranked 134th out of 135 countries in the world economic forum’s ‘gender gap’ report. according to the global climate risk index, pakistan is among the twelve countries most affected by climate change in the last twenty years, has suffered severe flooding and water shortages and is directly affected by the glacial retreat on the himalayas and karakorum ranges. pakistan is a semi-industrialised, lower- to middle-income country, with around one third of its population living below the poverty line. pakistan is ranked in 146th place among the 187 countries listed in the 2012 human development index (hdi), down from 145th place in the listing for 2011. the economic situation of pakistan has been harmed by successive natural disasters. pakistan has a high level of insecurity, instability and widespread corruption, which weaken its economic growth and limit the government’s ability to develop the state.
in writing. - strengthening pakistanʼs parliamentary institutions in their core functions of legislation, oversight and representation is a priority for eu-pakistan relations. i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i have voted in favour of this report, which calls for a resolution on an anti-missile shield for europe and its political and strategic implications, because i feel that an anti-missile shield is an appropriate european response to the current proliferation of nuclear weapons and other weapons of mass destruction. it is critical that a sense of security be maintained for european citizens and i believe that this shield would be a productive means of protecting european citizens from such threats.
in writing. - i agree with the intention to promote the idea of linking natoʼs ballistic missile defence to the eu’s common security and defence policy. i voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i supported this report, which discusses the situation and future prospects of the european fishing sector in the context of the free trade agreement between the eu and thailand. i agree that specific measures need to be taken in order to revitalise and ensure the future success of the currently-fragile european fisheries sector. a free trade agreement with thailand, one of the world’s leading fish-producing countries, will generate substantial benefits for the eu economy as a whole.

in writing. - gastronomy and cooking have become an increasingly important form of artistic and cultural expression. if nutritional food and gastronomic food are to fulfil their role in modern society, it is vital to create an appropriate education and culture. developments in recent years have highlighted that diet, in its broadest sense, is extraordinarily important in modern society. inappropriate diet is the main cause of conditions including obesity, anorexia, cardiovascular diseases, diabetes and gastric problems. poor diet is, of course, not the only problem. lack of exercise, consumption of harmful products such as drugs, excessive alcohol or tobacco, and a lack of emotional control are also major contributing factors in all these pathologies. the main objective of 21st century food culture and education must be to show and convince everyone, young and old, that it is not only possible, but also necessary, to combine healthy eating with gastronomy.
in writing. - i voted in favour of this report, which discusses in depth both the cultural and educational aspects of european gastronomic heritage. i agree that the european community must recognise the importance of diet in modern society and incorporate learning about diet and nutrition, as well as the knowledge and culture of food and gastronomy, into the various national education systems. making these changes will allow eu citizens to improve their health and reduce the amount of money pouring into public and private health systems for illnesses and conditions that could be prevented with proper education regarding food and nutrition.
in writing. - it is essential that learning about diet and nutrition, taste workshops and a knowledge and culture of food and gastronomy be incorporated into the various national education systems. i voted in favour of this report.

in writing. - the number of children involved in armed conflicts varies from 250 000 to 800 000. it is estimated that since 1996, 6 million children have been displaced by armed conflict, and some 2 million have been killed. some 20 million suffered human rights violations. every year, between 8 000 and 10 000 children are killed or disappear. the united nations declared 12 february as the international day against the use of child soldiers to raise awareness and to rally support for the zero under 18 campaign and to seek universal ratification of the optional protocol on the involvement of children in armed conflict. children are the present and the future. they therefore need protection and it would be a mistake for the eu to turn a blind eye to these shocking figures from around the world. i am calling for us to look around the eu and take action on children. i commend the maltese government for strengthening the country's laws. just over 12 months after the united nations committee on the rights of the child called for malta to ban the spanking of children altogether, a draft law amending the children and young persons act is being debated in the maltese parliament and 'for the avoidance of doubt, body-beating of any kind shall always be considered to be out of the question. 'by virtue of this amendment malta will comply with the european court of human rights. i call on the european parliament to also be a promoter of such causes.
in writing. - protecting children in armed conflict in accordance with international humanitarian law in order to avoid a lifelong devastating impact on their physical and mental development is a very important issue. i voted in favour.
