in writing. - the european union has set up legislative and budgetary instruments in order to provide additional support to workers who have been affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade patterns due to globalisation and to assist the reintegration of the same workers back into the labour market. the report cites how the number of jobs in southern hainaut of belgium has declined by 15.3% between 2007 and 2012, particularly in sectors where companies often employ a large number of workers. additionally, these workers will likely have to retrain to find alternative employment. therefore, i have voted in favour of the carsid application.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for the mobilisation of the egf in belgium for the support and reintegration in the labour market of workers made redundant in the production plant of carsid sa (‘carsid’). the fund will help 752 redundant workers to get back into employment through job-search assistance, training and retraining programmes.
in writing. - i have voted in favour of this report as it will improve the employability of the workers affected by these redundancies and support their reintegration into the french labour market. moreover, the financial assistance will provide support to workers made redundant as well as self-employed persons whose activity ceased as a result of major structural changes in world trade patterns.
in writing. – this application by france refers to 2 357 out of 6 120 workers made redundant at the peugeot citroën automobiles aulnay and rennes plant. the french authorities state that according to oica statistics, the eu-27 manufactured 16.2 million vehicles in 2013, out of a world total of 87.3 million (18.54 %), down from the eu-15 producing 17.1 million vehicles in 2000, out of a world total of 58.3 million (29.41 %). the commission has concluded that the application meets the conditions for deploying the egf. the commission has submitted a transfer request to the budget authority for a global amount of eur 12 704 605 for personalised services to be provided to workers made redundant. these include external experts guidance, thematic workshops, training, training allowances and grants for business creation. hence i voted in favour of this proposal.
in writing. - the european union has set up legislative and budgetary instruments in order to provide additional support to workers who have been affected by the consequences of major structural changes in world trade patterns due to globalisation and to assist the re-integration of the same workers back into the labour market. the report articulates how redundancies in the aulnay plant of france have reduced the number of employees therein by 13.6%. in rennes and brittany, numbers are less steep but are, nonetheless, still very significant. redundancies in the european automobile industry have been drastic overall. thus, the peugeot citroën automobiles application aims to help france reintegrate these workers, who have been made redundant in their industry as a result of globalisation, back into the labour market. therefore, i have voted in favour of this report.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund in france, which will mobilise the egf in favour of france to support the reintegration in the labour market of workers made redundant in peugeot citroën automobiles. the report concerns 2 357 out of 6 120 workers made redundant in peugeot citroën automobiles. the personalised services which are to be provided to workers made redundant consist of training and training allowances, among others.
in writing. – when i look back at the barroso ii commission there are notable positive features such as efforts in combatting child abuse and child pornography online. another positive was the introduction of incentives to increase the number of women in the it industry. others, however, are too negative to counterbalance these. tragedies in the mediterranean led the eu to create task forces, adopt packages and organise countless debates – but regardless of all this we had more immigrants dying in the mediterranean, and more eu citizens feeling disproportionate pressures. we still live in a union were 75% of asylum applications are processed in only 6 out of 28 member states. the barroso ii commission owed an obligation to its citizens to prepare itself for when the recession hit – but it did not. millions, particularly young people, are still unemployed while rising austerity measures and an increase in financial debt have left 120 million europeans at risk of poverty and social exclusion. most worrying of all is that during the last commission’s mandate the eu failed to connect to its citizens. i sincerely hope that the new commission manages to learn from these mistakes and makes a true difference for our citizens.
the european council and the commission have a challenge ahead of them, that of making policy that ensures affordable, secure and sustainable energy. and that is the balance we want to achieve, but to do that we need a complete and complete european gas and electricity network, linking together all the member states. there is an urgent need to end the energy isolation that some member states such as malta suffer from. projects of common interest designed to end this isolation should be considered a top priority for the european union. in an effort to diversify sources, routes and even suppliers, the european energy security strategy calls for a gas hub in the mediterranean. and yes, i believe that the mediterranean plays a crucial role in all of this. i therefore call on the european council to have the political will to make a climate and energy policy for the year 2030 that is ambitious and realistic and that focuses on energy security and measures to have the necessary interconnections in the mediterranean to end the situation of member states that are isolated and cut off from the european grid.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the new commission led by juncker since this is a commission that will be led by a president in a way that reflects also the vote of the people. furthermore, the commission´s work plan places social development high on its agenda, whereby economic growth is expected to be sought without excluding the social aspects. the commission is also expected to bring about investment in infrastructures related to education and employment.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the juncker commission as along with my political group, s&d, i believe that this commission can make a difference. this commission has also committed itself to support our agenda in addressing youth unemployment, enhancing competitiveness and focusing on energy and industry. we will hold this commission to this commitment. now it is time for the juncker commission to start delivering on what we have agreed upon including the eu 300 billion investment plan.
in writing. - this report intended to introduce the surplus to the budget, resulting from the implementation of the budget year 2013. due to the payment crisis, a historically low surplus of eur 1 005 million is to be entered in the 2014 eu budget. normally the surplus reduces the contribution of the members states to the eu budget by the same amount. i consider that this budget amendment is positive as it is taken in the best interest of the programmes in question that could have been negatively affected due to the lack of funds. that is why i supported this report.

in writing. - i fully support the budget of the european union for 2015, as we successfully managed to negotiated a package deal agreeing on targeted increases above the commission’s 2015 draft budget, especially for initiatives that will boost competitiveness, growth and jobs and will support initiatives in favour of reinforcements for asylum, migration and security. i also voted in favour of any amendments forbidding the use of any funds for breeders of bulls reared for bullfighting. the e.u funds should not be used to finance the exploitation of animals and their agony.
in writing. - i would like to register that my vote in favour of the motion is being made with great reservations. in the operation of the funds set under the annual budget, the union is implementing procedures of one size fits all. i will mention two examples from the experience of malta, the smallest member of the union, which is entirely an island. after malta, other countries joined as members. this had the statistical effect of lowering the average union gross domestic product per capita. malta thus lost access to certain structural funds. instead, the government negotiated and obtained additional financing under the current budgetary perspective. however, it is expected that small and medium-sized private farms will not benefit from these funds due to the union state aid rules. common sense tells you that the same rules should not apply to these funds. secondly, there is the case of gozo, a smaller island than malta with its own identity and special characteristics. it fell back due to double insularity. it needs regional leadership to develop. in an absurd comparison, the european commission decided against gozo being considered in this way by comparing it to the scandinavian islands. in the implementation of the european budget and the use of funds, an essential principle of what makes europe what it is is being forgotten - namely, a europe of nations. my vote is therefore conditional on the greatest possible reservations.
in writing. – this vote reflects the accession of croatia to the eu, which as a member state is to enjoy participation in the free trade agreement already in force between the eu and its member states of the one part and the republic of korea of the other. for this reason i voted in favour.
in writing. – the european union and the republic of korea adopted a mutual understanding on trade, known as the eu-korea fta, in 2011, with an majority of elected representatives from member states voting in favour of the agreement. trade agreements such as the eu-korea fta ensure guarantees and safeguards so that both parties reap the benefits. in order to safeguard trade agreements like these, the eu must ensure that new member states who join the eu fully participate in these forms of agreements. for this reason, i voted in favour of the reinstatement of this agreement taking into consideration croatia’s accession to the eu last year.
in writing. - i have voted in favour of this recommendation, as this additional protocol to the eu-republic of korea free trade agreement will safeguard the integrity of the union’s internal market. moreover, this recommendation will also make sure that croatia continues to fully participate in the eu-republic of korea free trade agreement.

in writing. - i voted in favour of the report on the european semester for economic policy coordination: implementation of 2014 priorities, which calls for the rapid implementation of a new investment plan of 300 billion promised by mr juncker and focuses on policy priorities in fighting poverty, precarious employment and youth unemployment.
as we have said in this parliament, any initiative and action on immigration must look at all aspects. we need to work more on solutions such as helping countries in africa get back on their feet, to go deeper into the possibilities of legal routes to europe so that people do not jump ship in fear and danger of their lives. there must be a safe return policy for those not eligible for protection. we must be tough, yes, in the face of evil networks that exploit vulnerable people and not target those who are the weakest. everyone saw the tragedy beyond our shores of hundreds of people being killed at sea without any scruples. yes, action is needed, but it is essential that any operation is carried out with full respect for human rights and the principle of proportionality. i note that the fundamental rights agency also felt the need to send practical guides on how to conduct operations such as mos maiorum. the message is clear: we must respect these guidelines in every operation, regardless of who is running this operation. i would also like to call once again for a spirit of solidarity between member states. i hope that such operations will not lead to the paradoxical result of an increase in the responsibility that countries like mine, namely malta, have to face under the dublin system. the ball is now in the council's and the commission's court and i would like to ask the council a question here, because it is represented here: will it help to ensure that member states finally fulfil their responsibilities in this area?
we welcome this intraschengen police operation as an important step towards the union gathering the information needed to ensure that we act more effectively in the face of organised crime which continues to empower us from the suffering of those who have already been victims of a cruel fate in their own country. as an operation that is expected to provide information identifying the main transit flows of illegal or irregular migrants by land, sea and air into member states, operation mos maiorum is a clear example of what cooperation in the fight against organised crime can bring. however, the information that this operation promised to gather reveals the need to improve the link between the union's internal and external policies on immigration-related issues. in the case of trafficking in human beings, this data is expected to provide information that should lead to better and more effective european policy and action. this operation is a few days, so now what next? in order to keep the union up to date with the situations it is facing, consistency in the collection of information on migratory inflows, together with the careful analysis of that information, is needed. operational support is also needed to member states such as malta that are most affected by immigration. operational support that goes hand in hand with financial support ensures a more effective use of that financial assistance and better results. judicial cooperation is also as necessary as police cooperation. in all of this, however, the union and the member states should ensure effective solidarity with full protection of fundamental rights, including during the deportation and repatriation process.
the presidency of the european council says that operation mos maiorum should tackle crime rings and also collect information on illegal immigration routes. but is that really the case? because i understand that this operation could lead to immigrants who entered a country and then went to other member states being sent back to the country where their application was processed. why is that? they are considered illegal, and in my opinion, with all due respect, this is not acceptable. this measure would make sense if this parliament's call to update the dublin regulations were taken seriously by the countries blocking this step within the council. as things stand today, this operation means that these people are sent back to the border countries. and on the one hand, who will help the border countries? and on the other hand, when will we see concrete proposals to address the tragic situation we have in the mediterranean? when will we have a single european response? and when an asylum seeker is not sent back to the country he first entered? our citizens are hungry for fair words and beautiful speeches on responsibility sharing. they want to see change, they want to see real equality and they want to see results in full respect for human rights.

in writing. – on 9 november we celebrated the 25th anniversary of the fall of the berlin wall and the iron curtain that divided western europe from eastern europe. this event earmarked the beginning of a new era of freedom, democracy and cooperation in europe. it also brought about a stronger sense of european unity towards which the eu plays an important role. in the last decade alone, we have extended these values and many central and eastern european countries have joined the union in 2004, 2007 and 2013. as europeans we must remain vigilant that we do not allow the creation of a new line of division in europe. the fight of those who brought about the fall of the berlin wall was founded on their right to determine their own destiny, their aspiration towards the rule of law and respect for human dignity. the same spirit and resolve that led to the fall of the berlin wall must continue to guide us as we move forward in our european project.
in writing. – i wish to express my deepest concerns over the outright challenge to the sovereign rights of cyprus. this provocative action of turkey seriously affects security in the eastern mediterranean region. tension is definitely on the rise, and unfortunately hydrocarbons are inseparably linked to the problem; they have escalated rather than de-escalated the situation. in my view this move of sending warships was mainly aiming to monitor a greek cypriot oil-and-gas exploration mission off the coast of cyprus. i believe that the republic of cyprus has the full and sovereign right to explore the natural resources within its exclusive economic zone. all eu actions should, no doubt, aim for defending international law and the sovereign rights of cyprus, a member state of the eu, and its eez. in fact, the discoveries of large hydrocarbon reserves in the eastern mediterranean and around cyprus should provide further impetus for a political solution to the division of the island.
in writing. – one cannot but fully support the maintenance of humanitarian action by the eu in south sudan. also its extension and strengthening. however, the time has come for the union, on an autonomous basis, to initiate and improve its intelligence-gathering and assessment capabilities in sudan and contiguous areas regarding: security; changing patterns of tribal and ethnic tensions; land use; raw material resource potential and contested claims to such resources; climate change; and most importantly perhaps, ongoing population movements, voluntary or not. such capabilities are at present deficient. on population movements, we need to know what the prospects are over the short to medium term. tension and strife in the sudan and contiguous areas have triggered bloody civil wars, dilapidated infrastructures, destroyed stable social structures and forced millions to find refuge away from their home. this has contributed to the disruptive pressures of illegal immigration across north africa and the mediterranean. we need a better understanding of the extent to which these pressures can develop, or to which they can be relieved, so that humanitarian and immigration commitments are made by the eu and nation states within a framework that our citizens will consider to be reasonable, prudent and enlightened.
mr president, in may the european citizen showed that he wants a change in the leadership of europe. perhaps he also wanted the signal for change to come from this parliament. almost six months after the european elections, i ask: the change our constituents wanted, is it happening? i say it is not. i expect that we will have clear debates on what the european people want, on where, in recent years, good decisions have been taken and where mistakes have been made in the governance of the european union and the euro area. i expected questions about how european unity is affecting the lives of the citizens who elected us, for better or for worse, so that where there is change, there is change. instead, we continued what was being done here before may. in a few countries managers have understood who has become president of a commission, of committees, of delegations and i don't know more. a few import them. the arguments in favour of more or less europe leave citizens blind to what causes economic stagnation and brings so many people out of work. we do not discuss why the european social model is falling apart and how we can build a serious immigration policy. this agenda has been hijacked by political extremists, and so-called populists. this is a big mistake. the challenges facing european citizens have not been overcome by the technocratic speeches we hear, as if we had become functionaries of the international monetary fund. the challenges are overcome by having a political discourse without corners, between representatives of the european peoples, including discussing from the bottom up the political structures that have been built before us if they are not serving europeans well.

in writing. – i have voted in favour of the mobilization of the european globalisation adjustment fund regarding andersen ireland and similar cases brought before this house, because workers who are being adversely affected by globalisation deserve support. were it not so, i would have voted against. globalisation is not only experienced when businesses close down or when they reduce their number of employees as a result of recession. beyond the effects of 2008, globalisation has led to large disruptions within and between nations. trade and investment have been disrupted in countries adjacent to russia and the so-called arab spring countries, including libya. the worst affected are small businesses that invested heavily in trade relations which today are undermined by warlike or terrorist activities against the backdrop of civil war. firms hit by these events, which lost jobs and business, should also be supported in the same way as businesses adversely affected by globalisation. the commission’s response to my question on this issue was most unsatisfactory. it is unfair that the workers of smes, which invested in libya for instance, and are now facing huge difficulties, are not treated the same way as companies and workers presently benefiting from the egf.

in writing. – this recommendation in essence focuses on moldova building a prosperous future within europe. whilst being aware that the implementation of the dcfta will require costly reforms, it can be seen that moldova is up to the challenge with the aim of modernising its economy and drawing in eu investments. in light of this recommendation benefiting both the eu as a bloc and the people of moldova i am voting in favour.
in writing. – i have voted in favour of the association agreement between the eu and moldova as it assists moldova’s choice for freedom and stability. the agreement will also strengthen the eu’s ties with the eastern neighbourhood. its immediate implementation is important for creating growth and jobs.
in writing. – i have given my consent to the conclusion of the association agreement between the european union and the republic of moldova because of the benefit to both sides and also as a sign of recognition of the reform efforts and ambitions of the moldovan people and authorities and of the substantial progress achieved in the latest period. i also join in the parliament’s call for free and fair elections in the country and for the upholding of human rights.
in writing. – this recommendation outlines the benefits that will result from the agreement between the eu and the european atomic energy community and their member states, in terms of energy security and decreasing energy costs. this agreement ensures transparency and efficiency, and aims to professionalise and depoliticise central and local public administration in order to aid in the implementation of the agreement. i have therefore voted in favour of this recommendation, which will have long-term benefits on the republic of moldova’s economy and the quality of life of moldovan citizens.
in writing. – i support the association agreement between the european union and the republic of moldova, which is a step in the right direction for the future relationship between the eu and moldova. the agreement will benefit citizens, businesses and consumers on both sides by providing better consumer protection and food safety, improved access to health services, more diverse and efficient use of energy resources and renewables and lower energy bills.

in writing. – the eu’s peace programme is aimed at strengthening progress towards a stable and peaceful society. the special eu programmes investment is shared between two funds – the peace iv programme, which aims to underpin efforts to build political stability and reconciliation, and interreg, which seeks to promote development and co-operation among the regions, including the republic, northern ireland and scotland. the peace iv programme has a strong youth element, concentrating on plans to enhance education and work training for young people and encourages greater levels of positive cross-community interaction. this deserves my support.
in writing. – this motion expresses concern at the fact that the implementation of the peace process in northern ireland has reached an impasse, and urges all parties to the process to work constructively towards a lasting resolution of the conflict and the full implementation of the good friday agreement and subsequent agreements for a long-lasting and enduring peace. this is why i voted in favour of this resolution.
in writing. – this motion notes the unfortunate impasse of the implementation of the peace process in northern ireland, and expresses concern for any continuation of violent, criminal and anti-social activity and the effect this might have on coming to a desirable agreement. it articulates the need to combat criminal activity through the improvement of economic challenges, including unemployment and low incomes, in hopes that this will encourage social stability. therefore, i have voted in favour of this resolution, which will encourage dialogue on this matter, so that parties will come to an agreement that will ensure enduring peace.
in writing. – i voted in favour of the resolution to support peace process in northern ireland, because i strongly believe that putting an end to all violent conflicts especially in the eu is essential for future prosperity and peace in europe.

president of the council. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, this is certainly an important moment, a timely moment to discuss the revision of the europe 2020 strategy and to discuss it in those priorities which are fundamental for the presidency, but which are also fundamental for the work, in general, that we are doing in the council. you know that the revision of the 2020 strategy has always been a priority of the italian presidency since the beginning of our semester, because we believe that this strategy must be relaunched and made more effective and because we believe that there is a dramatic level of economic and social crisis in europe to which the european institutions must give faster and more effective responses, primarily around horizontal strategies, such as the europe 2020 strategy. in your question and in your work you have raised a number of very important issues, and this is the very heart of the strategy. the 2020 strategy cannot be linked to one theme only, but it touches on many relevant themes to promote growth, to fight poverty, to create jobs. we are moving from an energy policy to a new knowledge policy and a sustainable development strategy. these are all topics that are linked horizontally when we talk about the new europe 2020 strategy. we wanted to include the discussion of mid-term evaluation and the identification of new priorities and the new method in all the formations of the council of ministers. and this was already a choice which was not only a choice of work organization but a choice of a clearly political approach. we believe that all the formations of the council of ministers must be active, proactive and contribute to achieving the results of europe 2020. we cannot continue - and this is a priority we have given to the functioning of the council of ministers in general, but it is particularly true of the europe 2020 strategy - we cannot continue to work with a council with compartments. we cannot develop large, mainstream, horizontal strategies covering various policies and then have a council of ministers that continues to work in stagnant compartments or that deals with europe 2020 in only one or two councils. no, this is the work we have to do, horizontal work. we have therefore discussed the main features of this strategy in all council configurations. it is a work which will be continued and finalised, as you know, by the latvian presidency which will be followed by the italian presidency. there are a number of points which have been made in the council. some fixed points which are first of all the objectives. we must maintain them and be more effective in achieving the objectives we set out in this strategy and which we have not achieved: the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. we have to be ambitious, more ambitious. there are positive results in some areas, for example in education. we have had absolutely unsatisfactory results, i would say very worrying, also as regards the credibility of who we are and the credibility of our work, as regards the fight against poverty, as regards the fight against unemployment. it is clear that we must proceed with greater determination and effectiveness. the third important message i want to give you is the issue of governance. it is clear that we also need to rethink the way in which the europe 2020 strategy has been managed, starting with a greater alignment of the europe 2020 strategy and the european semester. this is another area where i believe we must make a difference together in the new legislature. we cannot continue to have parallel processes in the economic, social and development fields that occasionally meet and occasionally talk to each other. it is clear that we must develop in a much more integrated way all those economic strategies which tend to achieve the results that you and we want, and above all the results of job creation and the fight against poverty. so we certainly need to do better to integrate the processes we have developed so far under the europe 2020 strategy and those we have carried out under the european semester, and this is clearly work that requires more dialogue and more interaction. we are working (some progress has been made and i believe we need to do much more) to coordinate the work that is being done in the ecofin area and the work that is being done in the employment and social affairs council. we know very well, and i do not need to remind this house, that this is also due to a lack of competence, to a difference in powers of competence. on the one hand we have a process managed by the ecofin council in the framework of the european semester, which is made up of binding processes and possibly also sanctions, on the other hand we are still in the so-called open method of coordination as regards the social aspects of work. it is clear that there is an imbalance that we need to work on and we can start working on it by integrating the policies and priorities that are being put forward in the social field more closely with the priorities and processes that are already well regulated in the ecofin framework. this is certainly a very important point. the issue of involving the social partners was also raised by mr ulvskog, as is the very important issue. increased involvement of social partners is essential for the success and strengthening of the social dimension of the europe 2020 strategy at national and european level. that is why we wanted to focus on the very subject we are discussing this morning at the trilateral social summit which we held on 23 october during our presidency. the process of consulting the social partners, however, needs to be streamlined to be effective. he can't have the long times you've had so far. so from this point of view we must make the dialogue between the social partners more streamlined, more effective and i would even say more interactive. we cannot limit ourselves to large summits of many hours in a day which then do not have the effect they should have in impacting the process as a whole. the other very important issue on which we need to do more and which we have identified as a priority is the evaluation of employment and social indicators. it is certainly a step forward, a good starting point. we are now giving greater visibility to the employment and social aspects. alongside this visibility, more effective concrete actions will also be needed. similarly, we need to make a much more determined commitment to gender equality. it is clear that the role of women in the labour market is one of the major objectives that we must continue to pursue in the europe 2020 strategy after its review next spring. i would also like to refer to a letter signed by 22 ministers which stresses this issue, stresses the need for gender equality to be reflected more strongly in the revised strategy. we as the presidency have fully supported the initiative of these 22 ministers. in conclusion, mr president, because i want to leave room for the speeches, i would like to mention the subject of investment, growth, recovery, structural reforms and investment. i am absolutely convinced that the real answer to the fight against poverty, along with all the things i have just mentioned, is a new investment policy. a new investment policy which, in our view, we shall see tomorrow, and the idea of the investment plan, an idea which was the idea of the italian presidency and which president juncker has wanted to adopt and which he will present tomorrow, so i cannot comment today on a plan which will be presented tomorrow. but, i think, we wanted a fresh start, we wanted a whole new beginning. juncker has committed himself to a new start, a new beginning. we believe that the investment plan is a good start, a positive start to develop an investment policy which must be based on european policies, in particular the europe 2020 strategy, but also on national policies which must be consistent with the new choice we are making at european level, namely to do what europe has been guilty of not wanting or not being able to do in recent years. what has been lacking in recent years has been an active european policy for demand, active for growth, through investment. now we have this new priority, and this is the real answer to fighting poverty, to creating jobs: public and private investment. tomorrow we will have a debate in the chamber, in which the presidency will also take part, but this, mr president, is certainly the most concrete, most tangible and most comprehensible answer for our citizens that we as the european union can give.
member of the commission. - madam president, since the europe 2020 strategy was adopted, we have indeed been moving away from the eu’s headline employment and social targets. for instance, the number of people living in or at risk of poverty has increased by more than 6 million. the economic and financial crisis has taken its toll: social hardship and the rise in equality call into question the fairness and effectiveness of our social market economy. however, the crisis should not make us overlook long-term social structural issues. it is accepted now that more and smarter investment in jobs, growth and competitiveness is needed. for this reason, the commission will present a eur 300 billion investment plan, which should mobilise money for much-needed projects to support job creation and improve living standards. i am happy that the council has already welcomed this wholeheartedly. as regards progress towards the europe 2020 targets, the modernisation of national labour markets and social protection systems is crucial. the european semester with the country-specific recommendations is a key venue for calling on the member states to make good on their commitments and deliver on their targets. the multiannual financial framework supports this ambition. a greater share – at least 23% – of cohesion policy funding has been ring-fenced for the european social fund, of which at least 20% will be specifically allocated to promoting social inclusion and combating poverty. the globalisation fund and the fund for the most deprived can also contribute to the employment and social targets. they support workers made redundant to find jobs, and they help the most deprived maintain a level of decency. both the existence and the distribution of these financial instruments were only secured with the very outspoken support of the european parliament. the youth unemployment initiative has been frontloaded in the first two years of the programming period, and we are working with the member states to get the funding on the ground. as to the new commission, there is an explicit agreement that employment and social policies and the related indicators need to be better taken into account in the european semester. indeed, they need to be better coordinated with economic and fiscal policies. as president juncker said, social fairness is as important as the stability of the single currency and the solidity of public finances. we must – and we will – also work more closely with social partners. as to the europe 2020 strategy, the commission is analysing the results of the public consultation, and it will present its follow-up in spring 2015.
on behalf of the ppe group. thank you, madam president. the eu's membership has reached an unacceptable level. and when considering youth unemployment, the consequences of the present situation should not be ignored. however, it would be a mistake to ask for the impossible, and to set unrealistic and binding targets at european level to try to solve this problem. the european people's party calls on the member countries to take the social field into account with the utmost seriousness and this should be done by identifying ambitious but realistic targets. one of the most worrying situations in many member countries is the increase in precarious work. it is important to address the abuse of these vulnerable persons. precarity is damaging the future of european citizens. it implies depriving these citizens of the peace of mind, which is their right, and increases the chance of poverty and social exclusion. the european social economy must be protected. member countries have watchtowers. care to reduce the abuse of those who are vulnerable. they must also be proactive and ambitious in implementing measures to ensure that citizens are offered work opportunities that guarantee their dignity and healthy development.
in writing. — i have voted in favour of this recommendation as its aim is to increase investment in the railway infrastructure and hence it will improve interconnectivity in europe. this recommendation also emphasises the need for a more sustainable and environmentally friendly mode of transport.
in writing. – i have voted in favour of this recommendation as i consider that the rail protocol is compatible with eu law and that the choice of legal basis for the proposal is correct. such an instrument is of benefit to the european rail industry, banks and governments by encouraging capital investment in the rail sector. i support the objective of the rail protocol, as it facilitates the financing of high-value railway rolling stock by creating strong international guarantees for the creditors. this should encourage not only capital investment in the rail sector, but also benefit the creation of a veritable lease market for mobile railway equipment throughout europe.
in writing. – according to the proposal the objective of the rail protocol is to facilitate the financing of high-value railway rolling stock by creating a strong international guarantee for creditors. the commission emphasises that this should encourage not only capital investment in the rail sector, but also benefit the creation of a genuine lease market for mobile railway equipment in europe. the rail protocol creates a register of international interests in railway rolling stock and is complementary to the european vehicle identification numbering system. it enables registration and search 24 hours a day using the internet. the proposal is thus closely connected to the 4th railway package, one of the aims of which is to encourage more innovation and investment in eu railways. as the proposal points out, the rail protocol is also consistent with the objective of supporting the shift to greener and more sustainable mode of transport such as rail transport. after having noted that this protocol creates guarantees for creditors, benefits the creation of a lease market and sustains a greener mode of transport i have voted in favour of this recommendation.
in writing. – this recommendation encourages the financing of high-value railway rolling stock, which will incite investment in the rail sector. this strategy will also include a shift towards a more sustainable and environmental friendly mode of transport. i have therefore voted in favour of this recommendation, which will facilitate the growth of railway infrastructure.
in writing. - i supported the report on the convention on international interests in mobile equipment on matters specific to railway rolling stock, because adhering to an international convention will provide strong international legal guarantees to creditors in relation with debtors in third countries, which is an important safeguard for eu producers and sellers.

in writing. – on 14 october 2014, the commission adopted a proposal for a decision on the mobilisation of the egf in favour of finland to support the reintegration in the labour market of workers made redundant in stx finland oy, operating in shipbuilding, due to major structural changes in world trade patterns as a result of globalisation. it refers to the mobilisation of a total amount of eur 1 426 800 from the egf for finland. it concerns a total of 565 beneficiaries. the commission has concluded that the application meets the conditions for a financial contribution from the egf, and the ep’s budgetary committee has already supported the decision with strong majority. hence i voted in favour of this proposal.
in writing. - finland aims to use these funds to provide 634 workers affected by difficulties in the shipbuilding sector which are related to globalisation with personalised services and to promote entrepreneurship. these workers are at risk of prolonged unemployment, since most of them have a relatively poor standard of education and are in an advanced age group. i have therefore voted in favour of this report in order to reintegrate these persons affected back into the labour market.
